# BT14 audit

## Status

The re-audit of 2026-09-10 reported all 102 BT14 cards at 10/10, recorded in `docs/audits/BT14-REAUDIT-LEDGER.md` (commit `93dfbc68c`) with an aggregate of 1020/1020 and executed collection, mechanism, effects-sync, typecheck, lint, format, and `git diff --check` gates. Where reports disagree, that newest dated ledger wins. The static pass of 2026-09-05 (`docs/audits/BT14-STATIC-AUDIT.md`) capped every card at 8/10 provisional because it deliberately executed nothing, and the closeout of 2026-09-02 (`docs/audits/BT14-AUDIT.md`) already claimed 10/10 before that independent re-audit ran. Both are kept per card as evidence, and every disagreement is listed under Open items.

Baseline: cumulative BT13 completion `0eb9dfb68f3fce67360d5f63e72895db02b386c9`.

Scoring: catalog/rules, IR trace, behavioral proof, peer/stack proof, delivery gates, each 0–2. Worker cap is 8/10.

Historical aggregate (2026-09-10): 1020/1020. The 2026-09-12 exact-name payment reproduction reopened BT14-090; the collection is no longer currently certified complete. A current aggregate requires recalculation after the reopened obligations are proved.

### BT14-001 — Koromon

**Final ledger.** Name: Koromon; Catalog: 2; IR: 2; Behavior: 2; Peer/stack: 2; Gates: 2; Score: 10/10; Status / evidence: Complete: semantic + focused + collection + delivery gates

**Static pass.** Catalog and rules: 2/2; IR trace: 2/2; Behavioral proof: 2/2; Peer and stack proof: 2/2; Executed gates: 0/2; Result: 8/10 provisional; Direct evidence: Natural opposing-security removal and inherited Once Per Turn proof

**Grouped semantic report.**

#### Catalog and KB clause trace

The immutable catalog record is a red Level-2 Digi-Egg with play cost -1, 0 DP, In-Training form, Lesser trait, and no evolution cost. Its inherited clause is `[Your Turn][Once Per Turn] When a card is removed from your opponent's security stack, ＜Draw 1＞.` The KB has no BT14-001-specific erratum, ruling, or banlist entry.

The clause requires the turn scope, a once-per-turn limit, the opponent’s security stack as the event source, and one draw for this player. It does not trigger when this player’s own security is removed, and the host must be a legal battle-area Digimon rather than a breeding-area card after the normal breeding restriction is applied.

#### IR, behavior, and peer/stack evidence

`apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-001.ts` compiles the inherited effect as a `YourTurn` `SubTrigger` for `whenSecurityRemoved`, with `sourceFilter.controller: opponent`, Draw-one for the host controller, and `OncePerTurn`; coverage is full with no residual gap. Registration is exclusively `registerIrCard("BT14-001", compiled)`.

`BT14-001.test.ts` contains a direction/once-per-turn source assertion (own-security removal does not draw; the first opponent-security removal draws; a second does not) and a natural production path: Koromon evolves to BT14-007, moves from breeding, attacks publicly, removes an opponent security card, and asserts the draw. This exercises the actual security extraction/event path rather than only injecting an event.

Peer and stack checks include the inverse security-removal watcher pattern in BT13-003 and the legal Koromon-to-Agumon stack used by the test. The shared trace is security extraction → `whenSecurityRemoved` payload → controller/source gate → turn/frequency wrapper → Draw.

#### Corrections, limitations, and score

No implementation correction was required. The source proof is adequate for positive direction, negative direction, once-per-turn, and a legal moved-out-of-breeding host.

| Catalog/rules | IR trace | Behavioral proof | Peer/stack proof | Executed gates | Provisional |
| ------------: | -------: | ---------------: | ---------------: | -------------: | ----------: |
|           2/2 |      2/2 |              2/2 |              2/2 |            0/2 |    **8/10** |

Limitation: runtime gates remain intentionally unexecuted by dispatch instruction; no unresolved card-specific behavior gap is known.

**Earlier pass (internal-docs/audits/BT14.md, 2026-08-28).**

#### Printed contract and knowledge base

- Catalog record: red level 2 Digi-Egg, In-Training/Lesser, no evolution requirements, inherited effect `[Your Turn][Once Per Turn] When a card is removed from your opponent's security stack, ＜Draw 1＞.`
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT14-001`: no card-specific Q&A, errata, restriction, or ruling. The comprehensive security-check timing is represented by the engine security pipeline, which publishes `whenSecurityRemoved` after the checked card leaves security.

#### Direct IR and shared primitive trace

- `apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-001.ts` contains one inherited `YourTurn` effect, frequency `OncePerTurn`, with a `whenSecurityRemoved` `SubTrigger`, `sourceFilter.controller: "opponent"`, and `Draw` 1 for `mine`. Coverage is `full`, residual is empty, and executable registration is exclusively `registerIrCard("BT14-001", compiled)`.
- `registration/module.ts` applies `withSubTriggerTurnScope` and `withSubTriggerFrequency`; these produce the `yourTurn` gate and an instance-scoped once-per-turn watcher key.
- `actions/subTrigger.ts` resolves `removedFromSecuritySeat` relative to the source owner. The explicit `opponent` direction rejects the controller's own security and accepts only the opposing stack.
- `security/securityCheck.ts` removes the revealed security instance and then publishes `whenSecurityRemoved` with the defending seat. `GameEngine.ts` preserves this as a pending trigger when removal occurs during another effect window.
- `actions/resources.ts` resolves `Draw` against the source owner's seat and moves the requested card through the engine draw primitive.

#### Peer, trait, and evolution-stack reasoning

- BT13-003 is the inverse-direction peer: it watches the controller's own security removal. Its tests confirm the same watcher seam, direction discrimination, and inherited once-per-turn behavior; BT14-001 intentionally uses `opponent` instead of that peer's `mine` gate.
- Koromon's Lesser trait is not referenced by this effect, so mixed-trait targeting is not applicable. The relevant evolution peer is BT14-007 Agumon, whose catalog and IR permit red level 2 evolution for cost 0 and the alternate `[Koromon]` route.
- The focused proof legally evolves BT14-007 over BT14-001 in breeding, verifies Agumon as the top card and Koromon as its inherited source, moves the stack to battle through the public intent, and performs a real player attack. The security removal draws exactly one card from Koromon's inherited effect.

#### Observable proof

- Positive/stack path: legal Koromon → BT14-007 evolution, breeding-to-battle transition, real opposing security removal, and one inherited draw.
- Boundaries: removing the controller's own security does not draw; the first opposing removal draws; a second opposing removal in the same turn does not draw again.
- Isolated command: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT14/BT14-001.test.ts` — 1 file passed, 3 tests passed.
- Revert lever: removing the opponent source filter, inherited marker, draw action, or once-per-turn frequency fails at least one focused observable assertion.

No ambiguity or unsupported behavior remains for BT14-001.

### BT14-002 — Bukamon

**Final ledger.** Name: Bukamon; Catalog: 2; IR: 2; Behavior: 2; Peer/stack: 2; Gates: 2; Score: 10/10; Status / evidence: Complete: semantic + focused + collection + delivery gates

**Static pass.** Catalog and rules: 2/2; IR trace: 2/2; Behavioral proof: 2/2; Peer and stack proof: 2/2; Executed gates: 0/2; Result: 8/10 provisional; Direct evidence: Inclusive source-count erratum and live Jamming boundary proof

**Grouped semantic report.**

#### Catalog and KB clause trace

The immutable catalog record is a blue Level-2 Digi-Egg with In-Training form and Lesser trait. Its inherited clause is `[Your Turn] While your opponent has no Digimon with more digivolution cards than this Digimon, this Digimon gains ＜Jamming＞.`

The KB records an erratum from “more” to “as many or more,” effective 2023-12-15, so the live comparison is greater-than-or-equal (`gte`). Q6004 confirms that when the opponent has no Digimon, the condition is satisfied. Jamming is the persistent keyword that prevents deletion in battles against Security Digimon; it does not grant general deletion immunity.

#### IR, behavior, and peer/stack evidence

`apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-002.ts` uses a `YourTurn` self `GainKeyword` effect for Jamming with permanent duration and `opponentHasNone` over opponent Digimon, comparing each opponent stack’s digivolution-card count to the source with `gte`. Registration is exclusively `registerIrCard("BT14-002", compiled)`.

`BT14-002.test.ts` structurally checks the errata-sensitive `gte` condition and behaviorally covers: no opposing Digimon (Jamming present), an equal-depth opposing stack (Jamming absent), and a legal blue Koromon-to-BT14-019 stack moved into the battle area where Jamming survives a losing Security Digimon battle. The test therefore covers both the threshold boundary and the actual security-battle keyword consumption path.

The realistic peer comparison includes BT14-029 and BT14-091’s comparable stack-count/Jamming logic and the BT14-019 evolution stack. The shared trace is live stack matching → `opponentHasNone` → permanent keyword application → Security Digimon battle Jamming check.

#### Corrections, limitations, and score

No implementation correction was required; the current IR reflects the catalog erratum, including the no-opponent case and equal-count boundary.

| Catalog/rules | IR trace | Behavioral proof | Peer/stack proof | Executed gates | Provisional |
| ------------: | -------: | ---------------: | ---------------: | -------------: | ----------: |
|           2/2 |      2/2 |              2/2 |              2/2 |            0/2 |    **8/10** |

Limitation: runtime gates remain intentionally unexecuted; the banlist/erratum is documented in the KB but does not require a source correction here.

**Earlier pass (internal-docs/audits/BT14.md, 2026-08-28).**

#### Printed contract and knowledge base

- Catalog record: blue level 2 Digi-Egg, In-Training/Lesser, inherited `[Your Turn]` Jamming condition based on opposing digivolution-card counts.
- The 2023-12-15 erratum replaces “more” with “as many or more.” The effective contract is therefore: this Digimon gains Jamming only while the opponent has no Digimon whose digivolution-card count is greater than or equal to this Digimon's.
- Q6004 confirms the condition is met when the opponent has no Digimon. The card is restricted to one copy from 2024-03-01; that deck-construction restriction does not alter runtime effect behavior.

#### Direct IR and shared primitive trace

- `apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-002.ts` is a hand-authored errata override with inherited `YourTurn` `GainKeyword(Jamming)`, a self target, and `opponentHasNone` over opposing Digimon whose `digivolutionCardsCompareToSource` is `gte`. Coverage is full, residual is empty, and registration is only `registerIrCard`.
- `conditions.ts` implements `opponentHasNone` by counting matching opposing permanents and requiring zero.
- `matching/permanent.ts` resolves the live source permanent's stack length and implements `gte` inclusively, so an equal opposing stack correctly suppresses Jamming.
- `actions/board.ts` resolves the self target and records Jamming in the continuous keyword ledger. `security/securityCheck.ts` consumes that keyword to prevent deletion after losing a Security Digimon battle.
- The `YourTurn` registration guard limits the continuous grant to Bukamon's controller's turn; inherited registration anchors the effect to the evolved host rather than the Digi-Egg as a standalone permanent.

#### Peer, trait, and evolution-stack reasoning

- BT14-029 Plesiomon and BT14-091 Wave of Reliability use the same errata-sensitive “as many or more” comparison vocabulary. Their `opponentHasNone`/comparison encodings agree with Bukamon's inclusive boundary.
- The Lesser trait is not used as a filter. Mixed board proof instead covers the material boundary: no opposing Digimon, a fewer-source opposing Digimon, and an equal-source opposing Digimon.
- The legal stack proof evolves blue level 3 BT14-019 Otamamon over BT14-002 for cost 0, verifies Bukamon remains the inherited source, moves it from breeding to battle, and pits its 1000 DP host against 12000 DP BT14-101 in security. With the opponent's only battle-area Digimon having fewer sources, Jamming is live and the host survives the otherwise losing security battle.

#### Observable proof

- Positive boundaries: no opposing Digimon satisfies Q6004; an opponent with fewer sources also permits Jamming.
- Negative errata boundary: an opposing Digimon with an equal source count suppresses Jamming, proving `gte` rather than the superseded strict-greater wording.
- Keyword consumption: the legally evolved low-DP host remains in battle after losing to a high-DP Security Digimon.
- Isolated command: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT14/BT14-002.test.ts` — 1 file passed, 4 tests passed.
- Revert lever: changing `gte` to `lte`/`eq`, omitting the condition, losing the inherited anchor, or removing the keyword grant fails focused assertions.

No ambiguity or unsupported behavior remains for BT14-002.

The trigger must be scoped to this player’s security stack, not the opponent’s, and can resolve only once during the player’s turn. “Added” includes a normal recovery/addition event, and the draw is for the host controller.

#### Printed contract and knowledge base

- Catalog record: yellow level 2 Digi-Egg, In-Training/Lesser, inherited effect `[Your Turn][Once Per Turn] When a card is added to your security stack, ＜Draw 1＞.`
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT14-003`: no card-specific Q&A, errata, restriction, or ruling.

- The focused proof evolves Elecmon over Tokomon in breeding, verifies Tokomon as the inherited source, moves the stack to battle, then publicly plays BT14-037 MagnaAngemon. Its real recovery adds the deck top to the controller's security and Tokomon draws the next deck card.

#### Observable proof

- Positive/stack path: legal yellow evolution, breeding transition, real recovery, security count increase, and exactly one inherited draw.
- Direction and frequency boundaries: an opponent-security addition does not draw; the controller's first addition draws; a second addition in the same turn does not draw again.

### BT14-004 — Tanemon

**Final ledger.** Name: Tanemon; Catalog: 2; IR: 2; Behavior: 2; Peer/stack: 2; Gates: 2; Score: 10/10; Status / evidence: Complete: semantic + focused + collection + delivery gates

**Static pass.** Catalog and rules: 2/2; IR trace: 2/2; Behavioral proof: 2/2; Peer and stack proof: 2/2; Executed gates: 0/2; Result: 8/10 provisional; Direct evidence: Natural own-effect suspension plus foreign-cause rejection proof

**Grouped semantic report.**

#### Catalog and KB clause trace

The immutable catalog record is a green Level-2 Digi-Egg with In-Training form and Bulb trait. Its inherited clause is `[Your Turn][Once Per Turn] When one of your effects suspends a Tamer, this Digimon gets +2000 DP for the turn.` The KB has no BT14-004-specific erratum, ruling, or banlist entry.

The cause must be one of this player’s effects, the suspended target must be a Tamer, the host must be this Digimon, and the DP modification lasts only for the turn and only once per turn. An opponent effect suspending this player’s Tamer must not qualify.

#### IR, behavior, and peer/stack evidence

`apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-004.ts` uses a `YourTurn` `whenEffectSuspends` sub-trigger filtered to source kind Tamer with `bySourceController: mine`, then modifies self by +2000 with `forTheTurn` duration and `OncePerTurn`. Registration is exclusively `registerIrCard("BT14-004", compiled)`.

`BT14-004.test.ts` has a natural stack and event path: Tanemon evolves to BT14-044, moves to the battle area, and public EX8-041 play naturally suspends an opponent Tamer, producing the qualifying effect event and +2000 DP. The source also probes a foreign/opponent-caused suspension before the qualifying event and asserts no DP increase, preventing source-controller overmatching.

Peer/stack evidence includes the BT14-044 Palmon evolution and EX8-041’s Tamer-suspension effect, plus comparable suspension-trigger patterns in BT10-004 and EX4-033. The shared trace is effect resolution → suspended Tamer event payload → source kind/controller gate → turn/frequency wrapper → temporary self DP modifier.

#### Corrections, limitations, and score

The current base already contains the necessary declarative-to-watcher correction: the effect watches actual suspension events and gates by source controller. This pass strengthened the negative foreign-cause proof; no further module correction was needed.

| Catalog/rules | IR trace | Behavioral proof | Peer/stack proof | Executed gates | Provisional |
| ------------: | -------: | ---------------: | ---------------: | -------------: | ----------: |
|           2/2 |      2/2 |              2/2 |              2/2 |            0/2 |    **8/10** |

Limitation: runtime gates remain intentionally unexecuted; source proof uses a natural EX8-041 cause plus a supplementary negative event probe.

**Earlier pass (internal-docs/audits/BT14.md, 2026-08-28).**

#### Printed contract and knowledge base

- Catalog record: green level 2 Digi-Egg, In-Training/Bulb, inherited `[Your Turn][Once Per Turn] When one of your effects suspends a Tamer, this Digimon gets +2000 DP for the turn.`
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT14-004`: no card-specific Q&A, errata, restriction, or ruling.

#### Direct IR and shared primitive trace

- The direct module installs an inherited `YourTurn`/`OncePerTurn` `whenEffectSuspends` watcher. `sourceFilter.kind: ["Tamer"]` restricts the suspended subject, `bySourceController: "mine"` restricts the causing effect, and the body modifies only the inherited host by +2000 DP for the turn. Coverage is full, residual is empty, and registration is only `registerIrCard`.
- `actions/subTrigger.ts` reads `effectSuspendSeat` for `whenEffectSuspends` and applies the causing-seat gate before resolving the subject filter. The generic permanent matcher distinguishes Tamers from Digimon.
- The registration layer carries `YourTurn` and the instance-scoped once-per-turn key onto the watcher. `ModifyDP` writes a turn-duration modifier for the self-resolved host, so it expires at the normal turn boundary.

#### Peer, trait, and evolution-stack reasoning

- BT10-004 and EX4-033 use the same suspension watcher vocabulary. Their implementations corroborate that “your effect” describes the causing seat and “a Tamer” describes the suspended subject; neither condition may be inferred from controller alone.
- Bulb is not used by the effect. The relevant evolution peer is green level 3 BT14-044 Palmon, which legally evolves from a green level 2 for cost 0.
- The focused proof evolves Palmon over Tanemon in breeding, moves the stack to battle, then publicly plays EX8-041 DarkTyrannomon. Its on-play effect suspends an opposing Tamer, publishing a real `whenEffectSuspends` event caused by Tanemon's controller; the inherited host gains exactly 2000 DP.

#### Observable proof

- Positive/stack path: legal green evolution, breeding transition, real effect-driven Tamer suspension, and +2000 current DP.
- Frequency boundary: a second matching event in the same turn does not add another +2000 DP.
- Subject and source gates are encoded directly and exercised through an opposing Tamer suspended by the controller's played Digimon effect.
- Isolated command: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT14/BT14-004.test.ts` — 1 file passed, 3 tests passed.
- Revert lever: removing the Tamer filter, causing-seat gate, inherited self target, DP amount, duration, or once-per-turn identity fails focused assertions.

**Static pass.** Catalog and rules: 2/2; IR trace: 2/2; Behavioral proof: 2/2; Peer and stack proof: 2/2; Executed gates: 0/2; Result: 8/10 provisional; Direct evidence: Exact-three trait cost, refusal, and Once Per Turn attack proof

**Grouped semantic report.**

#### Catalog and KB clause trace

The immutable catalog record is a black Level-2 Digi-Egg with In-Training form and Machine trait. Its inherited clause is `[When Attacking][Once Per Turn] By returning 3 cards with the [D-Brigade] or [DigiPolice] trait from your trash to the top of the deck, this Digimon gets +2000 DP for the turn.` The KB has no BT14-005-specific erratum, ruling, or banlist entry.

The attack is the event window; exactly three matching cards must be selected from this player’s trash and returned to the top of that deck. The cost is optional, so declining it must skip the return and DP processing. The resulting DP increase is temporary and once per turn.

#### IR, behavior, and peer/stack evidence

`apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-005.ts` compiles `WhenAttacking` plus `OncePerTurn`, an optional return-to-deck-top cost targeting three cards in the host controller’s trash and matching the OR trait set D-Brigade/DigiPolice, followed by self +2000 for the turn. Registration is exclusively `registerIrCard("BT14-005", compiled)`.

`BT14-005.test.ts` covers a natural qualifying attack with exactly three matching cards and a mixed trash containing a nonmatching card; it also covers the exact-three negative case with only two matching cards. This pass adds an explicit optional-decline case that asserts the three matching trash cards remain in place and the host does not gain DP. The legal stack uses Missimon over BT14-056 and the matching peer pool includes BT14-058 and BT14-060, with BT14-057 as a nonmatching control.

The shared trace is attack event → optional cost target resolution → all-three requirement and return to top of deck → temporary DP modifier. This confirms the trait cost is not a structural-only assertion.

#### Corrections, limitations, and score

No implementation correction was required; the current IR already modeled the trait OR-set, exact count, source trash, optionality, and temporary DP. This pass strengthened the source proof for refusal semantics.

KB Q2370 says a breeding-area host cannot use this effect; Q2371 says the normal digivolution requirements still apply; Q2372 says the digivolution cost must be paid. `BT14-006.test.ts` retains the invalid breeding-host and competing-source checks and the legal normal-cost path. This pass adds a natural causation proof: a BT14-071 Loogamon host is in the battle area, BT14-072 Fangmon is played from hand, and its real On Play effect returns BT14-074 Loogarmon from trash to hand and then trashes it. The shared trace is actual hand card move → `whenTrashedFromHand` payload with card/instance identity → mine-controller gate → source identity and trait match → battle-area legal digivolution candidate → normal payment.

#### Printed contract and knowledge base

- Red/green level 4 Dinosaur/Data Digimon, play cost 5, 5000 DP, evolving from red or green level 3 for cost 3. `[Start of Your Main Phase]` installs, for the turn, a 1-cost reduction whenever this Digimon would evolve into a Tyrannomon-named card or a Dinosaur/Ceratopsian-trait card. Its inherited `[End of Your Turn][Once Per Turn]` optionally attacks when the current Digimon has Tyrannomon in its name or Dinosaur/Ceratopsian among its traits.
- Q2375 says the start-main reduction persists and applies to every qualifying evolution in that turn. Q2376 says the inherited effect cannot make a suspended Digimon attack. Q2377 says its once-per-turn use does not reset if memory restores the same turn or if the Digimon evolves. Q2378 says two pending copies cannot both attack because the second resolves while the first attack remains active.

#### Direct IR and shared primitive trace

- Audit corrected two direct-IR gaps. The inherited gate had incorrectly treated `Tyrannomon` as a trait; it now uses `anyOf` with `selfHasNameContaining` Tyrannomon and `selfHasTrait` Dinosaur/Ceratopsian branches. The `Attack` now sets `drainTimingWindowDuringAttack: true`, so pending end-turn activations resolve during the declared attack as Q2378 requires. Coverage remains full, residual empty, and registration is only `registerIrCard`.
- The start-main `Replacement` is anchored to self, matches the destination definition through a name-or-trait `into` filter, and registers a non-consuming reduction in the turn replacement ledger. Digivolution cost calculation consults that ledger for every evolution. The attack condition reads the live stack host; the frequency tracker keys the inherited source instance for the turn. `forceAttack` enforces ordinary readiness, suspends the attacker, and, with timing-window draining enabled, rejects another attack while combat is active.

#### Peer, trait, and evolution-stack reasoning

- EX8-001 is the direct name-or-Dinosaur conditional peer and confirms that name and trait are distinct alternatives. BT14-016 Triceramon and BT14-017 Dinorexmon form the intended qualifying evolution chain and exercise Ceratopsian followed by Dinosaur. BT1-024 MetalTyrannomon is the decisive negative-trait peer: its Cyborg trait means the inherited attack can succeed only through the Tyrannomon name branch.
- The cost proof evolves BT14-013 into BT14-016 and then BT14-017 in one turn. The inherited proofs place BT14-013 beneath both a Tyrannomon-named Cyborg and a Dinosaur host, covering each alternative against realistic top-card identity.

#### Observable proof

- After start-main timing, evolving into BT14-016 costs exactly 1 instead of 2, then evolving again into BT14-017 costs exactly 2 instead of 3; memory moves 10→9→7, reproducing Q2375.
- A BT1-024 MetalTyrannomon host with BT14-013 beneath it attacks, suspends, and removes one security despite having no Dinosaur/Ceratopsian trait, proving the corrected name branch. Restoring it to unsuspended and firing the end-turn timing again leaves it unsuspended and security unchanged, proving Q2377's once-per-turn ledger.
- An already suspended BT14-017 Dinosaur host cannot attack or remove security, proving Q2376. With two eligible MetalTyrannomon stacks pending at the global window, exactly one suspends and exactly one security is removed, reproducing Q2378.
- Isolated command: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT14/BT14-013.test.ts` — 1 file passed, 4 tests passed.
- Revert lever: restoring the trait-only gate breaks the Cyborg host; consuming the reducer breaks the second memory assertion; removing timing-window draining suspends both hosts and removes both security cards.

No ambiguity or unsupported behavior remains for BT14-013.

#### Catalog and rules trace

The immutable record is a red Level 6 Mega/Data/Dinosaur/X Antibody Digimon, play cost 11,
11000 DP, with red Level 5 evolution cost 3. Its `[When Digivolving]` grants `＜Blitz＞`; during
all turns, while the opponent has one or more memory, the opponent cannot play Digimon with
6000 DP or less and this Digimon gets +4000 DP. KB Q2379 confirms the opponent-relative positive
memory perspective; Q2380 includes effect-played Digimon; Q2381 includes 6000-DP-or-less Digimon
tokens; Q2382 uses original printed DP even if an On Play effect would raise it; Q2383 checks the
restriction at declaration before payment crosses the gauge; Q2384 disallows paying without a

#### IR and runtime trace

`BT14-017.ts` separates the clauses correctly: `WhenDigivolving` grants Blitz for the turn;
`AllTurns` supplies a self +4000 aura and an opponent `RestrictPlay` for Digimon with printed DP
at most 6000, both conditioned on opponent-relative `memoryAtLeast(1)`. The RestrictPlay filter
now explicitly carries `allowTokens: true` for Q2381. Registration is exclusively
`registerIrCard("BT14-017", compiled)`.

#### Correction and shared seam

The audit found that generic `RestrictPlay` treated every token as exempt, while BT14-017/Q2381
explicitly includes Digimon tokens. The smallest reusable correction adds an opt-in `allowTokens`
field to the shared play match, preserves the default exemption for Q3834, passes the IR filter
through the RestrictPlay interpreter, and makes the token primitive consult the active prohibition
using the resolving effect's source seat. Thus a source player's effect can still play into the
restricted area under the normal source-seat rule, while a restricted player's own Goldramon
effect cannot create the matching tokens. A focused continuous-ledger test covers the opt-in and
the BT14 card test covers the natural effect path.

`BT14-017.test.ts` covers the natural hand-play prohibition, the +4000 DP live condition, natural
evolution across the memory gauge, the turn-scoped Blitz attack, the effect-played 6000-DP
boundary, and the positive-to-zero memory lift. The Q2380/Q2381 case was upgraded so natural
Goldramon hand play drives its own On Play token effect; the low-DP effect play remains in hand
and no Amon/Umon token enters. Shared continuous DP/restriction, memory perspective, token play,
and Blitz peers cover the mechanism and realistic Dinorexmon evolution stack.

#### Printed contract and knowledge base

- Red/yellow level 6 Holy Dragon/Four Great Dragons/Vaccine Digimon, play cost 12, 12000 DP, evolving from red or yellow level 5 for cost 4. On Play and When Digivolving each play 1 `Amon of Crimson Flame` red 6000 DP Rush Token and 1 `Umon of Blue Thunder` yellow 6000 DP Blocker Token. At All Turns, when Goldramon would digivolve or leave the battle area, it first deletes all of its controller's Amon/Umon; if that effect deleted a token, it performs Recovery +1 exactly once.
- Q2385 fixes the two printed token identities. Q2386 says tokens have no level or play cost and cannot satisfy filters requiring either. Q2387 says deleting at least one qualifying token performs Recovery +1. Q2388 says deleting multiple tokens still recovers only once. Q2389 applies the interrupt to every battle-area departure, not only deletion. Q2612 says BT16-014 Goldramon (X Antibody), after gaining this buried card's effects, immediately activates the gained When Digivolving effect.

#### Direct IR and shared primitive trace

- The direct module uses only `registerIrCard`. Its entry timings each execute one `PlayToken` action carrying both exact TokenSpec names and their Rush/Blocker keywords. Its two All Turns `Replacement` actions intercept self `wouldLeavePlay` and `wouldDigivolve`, execute one all-matching token deletion, then one `Recover` guarded by `ifThisEffectActed`. Audit removed the prior global `onDeletionOf` recovery watcher, which incorrectly recovered when an unrelated effect deleted a token.
- The canonical shared token table now defines Amon/Umon as red/yellow 6000-DP, level-less identities with play-cost sentinel -1 and exact printed effect text. Token play no longer remaps their printed names to obsolete `Amon Token`/`Umon Token` records. `definitionMatches` rejects a no-play-cost sentinel whenever a filter contains a play-cost constraint, reproducing Q2386 rather than letting -1 satisfy an `lte` comparison.
- `PlayToken` materializes both descriptors and projects their explicit keywords. Replacement resolution preserves action order and `ifThisEffectActed` state within the same body, so recovery is local to Goldramon's deletion batch and occurs once. The shared synthetic ID allocators now skip IDs already present in restored/seeded state; without that guard, the first token could collide with Goldramon's instance/permanent ID and spuriously recollect its On Play effect, duplicating the pair.

#### Peer, trait, and evolution-stack reasoning

- BT16-014 Goldramon (X Antibody) is the decisive evolution peer: its All Turns `GrantStatic grant: "effects"` matches the buried Goldramon name, confers this card's printed effects to the new top, and Q2612 requires the newly gained When Digivolving effect in that same real evolution window. BT14-015 is a legal red level-5 base for the ordinary evolution proof. Holy Dragon/Four Great Dragons/Vaccine are identity facts but do not filter Goldramon's own actions.
- The normal stack proof evolves BT14-015 into BT14-018 for cost 4. The gained-effect proof evolves BT16-014 over a seeded BT14-018 through its printed alternate Goldramon route for cost 2; the test automatically declines BT16-014's unrelated optional play effect so the simultaneous conferred effect resolves without a pending decision.

#### Observable proof

- A real hand play and a real BT14-015 evolution each create exactly one canonical Amon and one canonical Umon. Observations prove both tokens have 6000 DP, no level, no play cost, and live Rush/Blocker respectively; the evolution moves memory 10→6 and emits exactly one BT14-018 When Digivolving resolution.
- Deleting Goldramon with both tokens deletes the entire trio before departure and moves security 1→2 while consuming only one deck card, reproducing Q2387/Q2388. Returning Goldramon to hand produces the same one recovery and deletes Umon, reproducing Q2389. Attempting another evolution deletes Amon and recovers before the new top is installed. With no token, Goldramon leaves without recovery; deleting Amon directly while Goldramon remains also does not recover.
- A real BT16-014 alternate evolution moves memory 10→8, installs Goldramon X over BT14-018, creates exactly the two canonical tokens, and emits exactly one automatic BT14-018 When Digivolving resolution, reproducing Q2612 without manually firing a timing seam.

`BT14-030.ts` shares one bounce action object between On Play and When Digivolving. The Your Turn/Once Per Turn watcher listens for `whenDigimonReturnsToHand` from any controller, excludes MarineAngemon itself, and adds one deck card to the controller's security.

The shared return primitive routes Digi-Egg cards to the owner's face-down egg deck, removes tokens from the game while reporting successful processing, emits no ordinary hand-addition or Digimon-return event for those special routes, and publishes ordinary Digimon-return events for the recovery watcher. `apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-033.ts` is a hand-authored compiled IR override registered exclusively by `registerIrCard("BT14-033", compiled)`, with `coverage: "full"` and `residual: []`. The sequence searches all security to `revealed`, optionally self-digivolves from the prior search using `from: ["security"]`, `faceDownSecurityOk: true`, `amongPreviousSearch: true`, yellow Digimon plus Vaccine trait, and `payCost: false`; it then shuffles and optionally places a yellow Vaccine from hand at security bottom under the recognized `ifThisEffectDigivolved` condition. The declined case proves Patamon remains top, the searched security multiset is face down after shuffle, and the hand card remains available.

#### Printed contract and knowledge base

- Yellow level 3 Mammal/Data Digimon, play cost 3, 1000 DP, evolving from yellow level 2 for cost 0. At the start of its controller's main phase it searches security, may free-digivolve into a yellow Vaccine there, shuffles security, and if it evolved may place a yellow Vaccine from hand at security bottom. Its inherited Your Turn/Once Per Turn gains 1 memory when a card is added to its controller's security.
- Q2407 permits declining the evolution while still shuffling every searched card back. Q2408 requires the normal evolution bonus draw immediately after stacking. Q2409 delays the evolved card's When Digivolving activation until Patamon's whole effect has returned/shuffled the remaining security. Q2410 confirms the trailing hand-to-security placement triggers Patamon's newly inherited memory effect.

#### Direct IR and shared primitive trace

- The hand-authored IR has the required all-security Search, optional self Digivolve sourced from searched face-down security with no cost, shuffle, and conditional optional hand-to-security-bottom placement. The candidate filters are yellow plus Vaccine. The inherited watcher is controller-security `whenAddSecurity`, Your Turn/OncePerTurn, GainMemory 1. Coverage is full, residual empty, and registration is only `registerIrCard`.
- Search provenance limits the evolution pool; the effect-driven evolution stacks the security instance, performs the bonus draw, and defers its When Digivolving window until the parent action sequence completes. The trailing placement publishes `whenAddSecurity`, which the newly inherited Patamon source consumes. Audit fixed the shared security-placement Overflow seam: ACE cards moved from hand/deck/trash must not pay Overflow; only cards actually leaving a permanent or its stack/link do.

#### Peer, trait, and evolution-stack reasoning

- BT14-035 is the exact yellow Vaccine level-4 candidate and a legal evolution target. BT14-037 is a yellow Vaccine ACE used as the trailing hand placement specifically to prove that a loose ACE entering security does not falsely Overflow. BT14-033's Mammal/Data identity is not itself a filter.
- The resulting stack is BT14-033 beneath BT14-035; the test observes the retained inherited source, bonus draw, shuffled remainder, bottom placement, and memory trigger in one production sequence.

#### Observable proof

- Declining evolution leaves Patamon on top, preserves the exact two-card security multiset face down after shuffle, and does not consume the eligible hand card, reproducing Q2407.
- Accepting evolves into BT14-035 for zero memory, retains Patamon beneath it, draws the exact deck card, returns the remaining searched security, then places BT14-037 at security bottom. Patamon's inherited effect changes memory 0→1, reproducing Q2408-Q2410.
- The parent Patamon effect resolves before any BT14-035 When Digivolving event when such an event exists, preserving Q2409 ordering. The placed BT14-037 ACE causes no Overflow because it moved from hand rather than leaving play.
- Isolated command: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT14/BT14-033.test.ts` — 1 file passed, 3 tests passed. Affected mechanisms: `primitives.test.ts` 130/130 and `overflow.test.ts` 14/14.
- Revert lever: dropping security/search flags prevents evolution; making it mandatory fails Q2407; charging evolution changes memory/draw; firing the child early reverses event order; broad Overflow application changes final memory to -2; losing the inherited watcher leaves memory at 0.

No ambiguity or unsupported behavior remains for BT14-033.

Return the revealed cards to the top or bottom of the deck.` The local query returned Q2437 and Q2438. Q2437 is explicit that three revealed Digimon cause three repeated De-Digivolve 1 actions, not one De-Digivolve 3 action; the process stops at level 3 or when the stack is exhausted. Q2438 confirms that the player activating the effect chooses the order and top/bottom destination.

#### Implementation and shared-runtime correction

`apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-065.ts:8-77` carries opponent-controlled RevealAdd for both triggers and a De-Digivolve 1 action scaled by one unit for each revealed Digimon. The module previously used `rest: "deck"`, which was not a valid semantic disposition and fell through to bottom-deck behavior, and `runRevealAdd` previously always revealed the source owner's deck even when the action carried `controller: "opponent"`.

This audit makes a production/shared-runtime correction: `packages/shared/src/effects/ir/actions/reveal.ts` now declares `RevealAddAction.controller?: Controller`; `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/reveal.ts` routes `opponent` to the opposing deck (and supports `any` through the normal choice seam); and BT14-065 uses `rest: "deckTopOrBottom"` for both triggers. This is a shared runtime correction, not merely a test fixture adjustment. `runRevealAdd` stores the revealed pool in `lastRevealedCards`, which `scaleFactor` reads for the repeated De-Digivolve count; `primitives.ts` then promotes one source per repetition. Registration remains the sole `registerIrCard("BT14-065", compiled)`.

#### Behavioral, peer, and stack evidence

`apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-065.test.ts:19-41` naturally plays Vademon, reveals three opponent Digimon, chooses the top return branch, and proves three repeated De-Digivolve 1 promotions on an opponent BT14-067 stack. Lines 43-73 naturally evolve a black BT14-061 into Vademon and prove the separate When Digivolving trigger, one scaled peel, and top-deck restoration.

The BT14-061 -> BT14-065 evolution is a realistic black level-4-to-level-5 stack. The repeated De-Digivolve order follows the engine's `popFromStack` behavior and the adjacent BT14-067 reveal-budget/top-or-bottom implementation was compared as a peer. Both Q2437 and Q2438 are represented by natural play/evolution paths and a real choice responder, although no test was run.

#### Corrections, limitations, and score

Production/shared-runtime correction: opponent reveal ownership and top-or-bottom rest semantics were fixed in the shared IR trace/runtime seam and the card module. Natural On Play and When Digivolving proofs now cover the complete printed behavior. Tests, typecheck, lint, formatter, browser, mechanism, collection, and diff gates remain unexecuted. #### Implementation and runtime

`apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-068.ts:8-80` encodes a seven play-cost `DeleteBudget`, an OpponentsTurn all-own-D-Brigade `GainKeyword` Blocker grant, and an EndOfYourTurn Once Per Turn RevealAdd with a seven total play-cost budget and trash remainder. `actions/removal.ts` accumulates DeleteBudget target costs; `continuous.ts` derives the OpponentsTurn grant and clears/recomputes turn scope; `runRevealAdd` enforces summed free-play budgets and runs each selected card's On Play lifecycle. Lines 60-102 naturally attack the player during the opponent's turn, prove a separate D-Brigade BT14-056 host is an eligible Blocker, declare the block, and keep security safe; after changing to the owner's turn and recomputing, the same host no longer has Blocker (`observe.hasKeyword(...) === false`). This negative assertion specifically guards the printed opponent-turn scope against the IR's `duration: "permanent"` field under the OpponentsTurn trigger. Lines 104-126 drive a real `advance(...).runTurn(0)` through End of Your Turn, reveal BT14-060 plus the DigiPolice Tamer BT14-086 within the seven-cost sum, and confirm the rest is trashed; this directly covers Q2441's Tamer clause.

The BT14-064 -> BT14-068 evolution is a realistic black level-5-to-level-6 stack. BT14-056 is a D-Brigade host without its own Blocker, so the positive attack proves the grant rather than an inherited keyword; BT14-060 and BT14-086 are realistic D-Brigade/DigiPolice free-play peers. The end-turn case uses the real turn loop and `EndOfYourTurn` production timing, not a direct fire injection.

### BT14-101 — WarGreymon

**Final ledger.** Name: WarGreymon; Catalog: 2; IR: 2; Behavior: 2; Peer/stack: 2; Gates: 2; Score: 10/10; Status / evidence: Complete: semantic + focused + collection + delivery gates

**Grouped semantic report.**

#### Catalog and rules

The immutable entry identifies a red level 6 Digimon, play cost 12, 12000 DP, Vaccine/Dragonkin,
with the ordinary red level 5 evolution cost 4. Its printed clauses are: conditional hand evolution
of one Agumon for 4 while a Tamer named Tai Kamiya and an opposing Digimon at 10000 DP or more are
present; When Digivolving, gain Raid for the turn and optionally attack; and When Attacking, if a
Tamer is present, gain Security Attack +1 and Piercing for the turn.

The separate local KB query was `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT14-101`. Q2484 (2024-03-28) confirms
that the conditional effect may digivolve an Agumon in the battle area into BT14-101 in hand when the
Tai Kamiya and opposing 10000-DP conditions are satisfied.

Rules trace: comprehensive rules §15-7 (the optional processing condition and its dependent action),
§15-16 (When Digivolving/When Attacking timing), §16-23 (Raid selects the highest-DP unsuspended
opposing Digimon, with controller choice on ties), §16-4 (Security Attack +1), §16-7 (Piercing),
and §11-1 (attack target removal/attack lifecycle). The local KB text also records the same timing,
Raid, security, and Piercing semantics.

#### Direct IR and runtime trace

`apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-101.ts:8-72` carries the conditional `Main`/`isFromHand` effect, the
Tai Kamiya name filter, the opponent DP `gte 10000` filter, Agumon target, hand source, `costOverride: 4`,
`payCost: true`, `ignoreRequirements: true`, and optional abort behavior. The normal alternate
requirement remains `Lv.5` with Greymon in the name at cost 4 (`:162-168`).

`apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-101.ts:75-105` carries the ordered When Digivolving Raid grant and
optional self-Attack. `:107-157` carries both Tamer-gated, for-the-turn When Attacking grants.
The module ends with the sole registration at `:172`.

The hand activation is a real public `activateEffect` intent. The hand source is surfaced through the
OnDeclaration hand path (`apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-101.test.ts:12-15`); the builder's residency
guard is in `apps/api/src/engine/effects/builders.ts:151-168` and its hand-activation contract is
documented at `:240-245`. `registerIrCard` normalizes the direct module and installs it as executable
IR at `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/registration/module.ts:323-340`.

The digivolve interpreter first finds a hand source and matching Agumon, then honors
`ignoreRequirements` separately from payment (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/digivolve.ts:135-183`;
`:300-400`). The real combat controller fires the When Attacking timing before battle and applies Raid
to the highest-DP unsuspended opponent (`apps/api/src/engine/combat/controller.ts:575-590`, `:634-661`).
GainKeyword stores ordinary temporary keywords in the continuous ledger and Piercing in its dedicated
consume store (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/board.ts:260-355`).

#### Behavior-driving source proof

`apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-101.test.ts:42-90` now drives the natural public origin:

1. A hand-resident BT14-101 is activated with a Tai Kamiya Tamer and an opposing 11000-DP Digimon.
2. The target is a bare Agumon, so the resulting stack proves the 4-memory, requirement-ignoring path.
3. The When Digivolving attack starts naturally; Raid chooses the highest unsuspended target (BT1-025)
   while leaving the lower-DP BT14-069 in play.
4. The test observes Raid, Security Attack +1, and Piercing, and uses the winning battle plus Piercing
   to require two security checks. It also asserts the hand source left hand and exactly 4 memory was paid.

This source proof is intentionally unexecuted under the task policy; no natural-origin limitation was
encountered, so the behavioral component is retained at 2/2 provisionally.

#### Peer and stack proof

The hand/evolution shape follows the natural hand-evolution peers in `BT14-007.test.ts` and

and deliberately proves that the bare Lv.3 base succeeds only because this effect ignores the normal

### BT14-102 — Angemon

**Final ledger.** Name: Angemon; Catalog: 2; IR: 2; Behavior: 2; Peer/stack: 2; Gates: 2; Score: 10/10; Status / evidence: Complete: semantic + focused + collection + delivery gates

**Static pass.** Catalog and rules: 2/2; IR trace: 2/2; Behavioral proof: 2/2; Peer and stack proof: 2/2; Executed gates: 0/2; Result: 8/10 provisional; Direct evidence: Natural modal deletion, self placement/hatch, and inherited Security proof

**Grouped semantic report.**

#### Catalog and rules

The immutable entry identifies a yellow level 4 Digimon, play cost 6, 6000 DP, Vaccine/Angel, with
the alternate Patamon evolution cost 2. Its clauses are: When Attacking, by deleting itself, activate
one of two effects (place an opponent's Virus Digimon at the bottom of security, or give one opposing
Digimon -5000 DP for the turn); On Deletion, place this card at the bottom of its owner's security,
then, if a Tamer is present, optionally hatch; and inherited On Deletion, optionally place one yellow
Vaccine card from hand at the bottom of its owner's security.

The separate local KB query was `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT14-102`. Q2485 confirms self-deletion
is legal even with no opposing Digimon. Q2486 confirms hatching is unavailable when the breeding area
is occupied. Q2487 confirms self-placement in security still occurs when hatching is unavailable.
Q2488 confirms the inherited hand placement is optional when the eligible card is in a private hand.

Rules trace: comprehensive rules §15-7 (by-cost optional processing and modal follow-up), §15-8
(triggered/deletion timing), §15-16 (When Attacking/On Deletion), §3-4-7 (single breeding slot),
§3-1-3-5 (bottom-security placement), and the local KB's Q2485–Q2488 interpretations.

#### Direct IR and runtime trace

`apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-102.ts:7-56` carries the When Attacking modal, its `deleteOwn` cost,
the Virus bottom-security branch, the -5000 branch, and optional abort semantics. `:59-85` carries
self bottom-security placement followed by the Tamer-conditioned optional Hatch. `:87-108` carries
the inherited, optional, hand-sourced yellow Vaccine placement. The module ends with the sole
registration at `:121`.

The generic action runner pays the `deleteOwn` action cost before resolving the modal and aborts the
remaining clause if payment fails (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/runAction.ts:454-531`).
Modal resolution selects exactly one available branch (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/modal.ts:13-87`).
`SecurityManipulation.placeAsSecurity` resolves both field targets and loose hand targets, and uses
`toTop: false` for bottom placement (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/security.ts:288-407`).
The Hatch action delegates to the single-slot hatch primitive (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/board.ts:146-153`),
while the optional Hatch preflight suppresses a no-op when breeding is occupied or the egg deck is empty
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/runAction.ts:314-320`).

Deletion fires the on-deletion bus before movement and then opens the post-movement On Deletion window
with the actual deleted instance set and stack-card subset (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/primitives.ts:3013-3043`,
`:3167-3229`). The On Deletion builder admits the deleted source and distinguishes inherited stack
cards from the deleted top card (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/builders.ts:171-197`). This is the seam
that makes the inherited BT14-102 source resolve after a natural security-battle deletion.

#### Behavior-driving source proof

The new source proofs use only public attack intents and real settlement:

- `apps/api/src/cards/BT14/BT14-102.test.ts:30-66` accepts modal branch 0, naturally deletes Angemon,
  places the Virus target at the opponent's security bottom, places Angemon at its own security bottom,
  and hatches a real Digi-Egg with a Tamer.
- `:69-97` selects modal branch 1, naturally deletes Angemon, preserves the opposing target, and observes
  the exact 7000 DP result from -5000.
- `:100-132` keeps a Tamer and egg deck but occupies breeding, proving the security placement remains
  while the hatch cannot move an egg into the occupied slot.
- `:135-161` attacks a Seraphimon host carrying BT14-102 into a 15000-DP security Omnimon. The host
  loses by the public security battle, and the inherited effect naturally selects BT14-035 Unimon
  (yellow Vaccine) from hand and places it at the bottom of security.

These tests are source evidence only because execution was explicitly prohibited. The natural public
origins are supported by the current harness/runtime, so no behavioral deduction was necessary.

#### Peer and stack proof

The self-security placement and inherited placement shapes are compared against `BT14-038.test.ts`
(`:59-81`), while the Hatch decision and occupied-breeding behavior follow `BT8-091.test.ts`.
The inherited proof uses a realistic `[BT14-041 Seraphimon, BT14-102 Angemon]` stack and a legal
yellow Vaccine hand card (`BT14-035 Unimon`), with the host's natural security battle supplying the
deletion event. The modal branch 1 target uses BT14-041 at 12000 DP, so -5000 remains observable at
7000 without a zero-DP deletion side effect.

#### internal-docs/audits/BT14/BT14-001-010.md

##### BT14 static card-by-card re-audit: BT14-001 through BT14-010

###### Audit frame

This report independently re-audits exactly BT14-001 through BT14-010 in ascending card-ID order. The immutable catalog source was Git blob `efbecc002fb9000789123e2f91f201466e1e5b0a`; the local rules/errata/QA knowledge base was queried for every card. Historical `internal-docs/audits/BT14.md` was treated as prior evidence only and was not used as a substitute for recalculation.

The direct card modules, current interpreter and primitives, colocated source tests, realistic peer/evolution stacks, and registration paths were inspected. All ten modules currently register executable behavior only through `registerIrCard(cardId, compiled)`. No generated snapshot, collection ledger, or protected `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` file was changed.

Per the dispatch constraints, no tests, typecheck, lint, formatter, browser/UI check, collection/mechanism gate, or `git diff --check` was executed. Therefore executed gates are fixed at 0/2 and every score below is provisional, with a maximum of 8/10. “Behavioral proof” means the source tests contain behavior-driving assertions and natural originating-event paths; it does not claim those tests were run.

###### Shared rules and interpreter trace used throughout

- The KB’s comprehensive rules sections `3-4-7-3` through `3-4-7-7` establish that breeding cards are not normally affected or chosen and that effects do not trigger there unless a breeding-area exception is explicit. Sections `7-1-1` through `7-1-3` cover playing cards and normal hand-to-battle-area play; `8-1-1` through `8-1-3` cover digivolution requirements, orientation, and payment. Sections `15-5` through `15-8` cover trigger conditions, processing conditions, optional processing, and persistent effects. Manual sections `16-8` and `16-9` cover Draw and Jamming.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/effect.ts` supplies turn-scope and frequency wrappers; `registration/module.ts` applies them to registered triggers. `subTrigger.ts` enforces controller, source-kind, turn, once-per-turn, hand-trash, and effect-suspension gates. `conditions.ts` and `matching/permanent.ts` provide live field/stack matching. `continuous.ts` and `actions/restrictions.ts` preserve restriction provenance, including `byEffectOnly`; `primitives.ts` performs play, digivolution, trash, and security event dispatch. `security/securityCheck.ts` extracts security cards, fires security-removal events, and applies Jamming only in the Security Digimon battle path.
- Source tests were inspected but intentionally not executed. Exact test names and paths are recorded per card so a later runtime audit can reproduce the evidence and fill the fixed 0/2 gate component.

###### Corrections and delivery record

Source/proof corrections made in this pass:

- BT14-004: added source-test coverage proving an opponent-caused Tamer suspension does not qualify.
- BT14-005: added source-test coverage for declining the optional exact-three return cost.
- BT14-006: added a natural Fangmon-originated hand-trash path that preserves the trashed card identity and pays the normal evolution cost.
- BT14-009: added named production-primitive coverage for effect-play into breeding and for restriction persistence on a legal evolution stack.

No shared interpreter/primitives change was needed, and no unresolved implementation limitation remains for BT14-001 through BT14-010. The full range is 10 cards, each provisionally 8/10 under the mandated fixed executed-gate score of 0/2; the range is not a collection completion claim.

Exact commits:

- Audit base: `f7fd69086a115461112f5588ff8801a49e406669`.
- Source/proof commit: `8bd0d9dbf3fa8a73db05fd6b89ba7b4898c1b6be` (`test(BT14): strengthen cards 004-009 audit proofs`).
- Source/proof commit: `78538c617f6e20a596be33eddbdd8424c187b44c` (`test(BT14): cover Gotsumon evolution-stack restriction`).
- Documentation creation commit: `ee962306db47ab69b8919ca2e69eb8cfeef4a05a` (`docs(BT14): record static audit for cards 001-010`).

The branch is to be pushed normally after the focused documentation commit(s). The next queue is BT14-011.

#### internal-docs/audits/BT14/BT14-011-020.md

##### BT14 Static Card-by-Card Re-Audit: BT14-011–BT14-020

Audit date: 2026-08-28  
Base: `f7fd69086` (`Open BT14 static audit`)  
Immutable catalog blob: `efbecc002fb9000789123e2f91f201466e1e5b0a`  
Range: 10 cards, exactly BT14-011 through BT14-020  
Next queue: BT14-021

###### Scope and evidence method

This is a fresh static re-audit. The catalog record for every card was read directly from the
immutable blob with `git cat-file blob efbecc002fb9000789123e2f91f201466e1e5b0a`, and the local
KB was queried in ascending order with `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT14-NNN`. Historical
`internal-docs/audits/BT14.md`, `BT14-STATIC-AUDIT.md`, and the prior ledger were used only to
locate context; their earlier scores were not reused.

The review covered each direct module, its registered IR, the shared interpreter and primitives,
the colocated behavior tests, the registration path, and realistic peers/evolution stacks. The
card modules all have `coverage: "full"`, `residual: []`, and register exactly once through
`registerIrCard(cardId, compiled)`. No audited module retains a duplicate `registerCard` call.

The score is five components worth two points each: catalog/rules, IR trace, behavioral proof,
peer/stack proof, and executed gates. Per the dispatch constraint, tests, typecheck, lint,
formatters, browser/UI checks, collection/mechanism gates, and `git diff --check` were not run;
the executed-gates component is therefore `0/2` for every card. These are provisional static
scores with a maximum of `8/10`, not collection completion evidence.

###### Protected generated snapshot

`packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` was inspected but not changed. It is stale for parts of
BT14-012, BT14-013, BT14-017, BT14-018, and BT14-020 (for example, older raw conditions, omitted
trait alternatives, the pre-correction Dinorexmon placement, bare Goldramon token descriptors,
and an incomplete Goldramon replacement). The executable path imports `apps/api/src/cards/BT14/index.ts`,
whose side effects call `registerIrCard`; `registration/module.ts` normalizes the direct module
and updates `registeredCompiledCards`/the runtime `compiledEffects` entry. The direct module is
therefore the runtime authority after registration. The protected snapshot remains a documented
follow-up limitation rather than an unauthorized file mutation.

#### internal-docs/audits/BT14/BT14-051-060.md

###### Method, runtime seams, and gate status

For every card, the immutable catalog record was reread for color, kind, level, play cost, DP, every evolution requirement, form, attribute, trait, effect text, inherited text, Security text, rarity, maximum copies, and ACE/Overflow metadata where applicable. Each card was queried in the local rules KB, then its direct compiled module, colocated test, shared runtime, neighboring peers, realistic evolution/stack shapes, and registration path were inspected. Every direct module below has `coverage: "full"`, `residual: []`, and exactly one executable registration of the form `registerIrCard("BT14-NNN", compiled)`; no audited module has a duplicate `registerCard` registration.

The shared runtime paths inspected were:

- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/registration/module.ts:44-120` for compiled module normalization, trigger routing, and registration.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/compiledCards.ts:10-43` for registered compiled cards and universal Rule-name aliases.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/subTrigger.ts:422-434, 788-805, 898-906` for self-scoped versus filtered `whenEffectSuspends`, attack event subjects, and leave-cause gates.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/replacement.ts:186-255` for prevention replacements and `otherThanYourEffect` cause matching.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/placeUnder.ts:29-47, 145-180` for Save-style placement and stack relocation.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/reveal.ts:108-188, 808-809` for reveal/add/play, partial candidate counts, and deck-bottom routing.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/effect.ts:96-121` and `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/combat.ts:1-55` for When Attacking, turn-end, and forced-attack routing.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/primitives.ts:3325-3350` and `apps/api/src/engine/combat/controller.ts:1333-1350` for effect-suspension and battle-deletion event origins, including inherited leave-play watchers.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/conditions.ts:899-916` was inspected and deliberately left unchanged. The temporary global `universalNameAliasesFor` addition was reverted: none of BT14-051 through BT14-060 needs `selfHasNameContaining` to consult a universal Rule-name alias. BT14-052's inherited check is proved with a legal stack ending in printed-name `BT4-061 BanchoLeomon`, while BT14-060's executable clauses use trait filters and leave-play replacement rather than that condition. Consequently, there is no audited behavior that should fail without this global change, and introducing a synthetic alias-only test would misrepresent the scope.

The five scoring components are each worth two points: catalog/rules fidelity, executable IR trace, behavior-driving proof, peer/stack proof, and executed gates. Structural assertions and manual timing/event injection were not counted as full behavior where a natural origin was available. No tests, typecheck, lint, formatter, browser check, collection gate, mechanism gate, or `git diff --check` was run, as required by the dispatch. Therefore executed gates are fixed at `0/2` and every maximum is provisional at `8/10`; colocated tests cited below are source evidence present in the worktree, not executed evidence.

#### internal-docs/audits/BT14/BT14-061-070.md

##### BT14 Static Card-by-Card Re-audit — BT14-061 through BT14-070

Audit date: 2026-08-28
Audit base: `9acedd447` (`Assign BT14-061 through BT14-090 audit`) on the source branch `audit-bt-card-by-card`
Worker branch: `audit-bt14-061-070-luna`
Scope: exactly BT14-061, BT14-062, BT14-063, BT14-064, BT14-065, BT14-066, BT14-067, BT14-068, BT14-069, and BT14-070, audited in strict ascending order.
Catalog authority: immutable Git blob `efbecc002fb9000789123e2f91f201466e1e5b0a`, reread directly with `git cat-file blob`.
Knowledge-base authority: `node tools/kb/query.mjs card <CARD-ID>` and the referenced local KB/rules sources.

###### Method, score model, and global findings

For each card I reread the complete immutable catalog record: identity, colors, kinds, level, play cost, DP, every evolution requirement, form, attribute, traits, printed effect text, inherited effect text, Security text where present, rarity, and maximum count. I queried the local KB separately per card, inspected the direct TypeScript module and colocated test, traced each shared interpreter/runtime seam used by the card, and compared realistic peers and evolution/stack shapes. The colocated tests below are source proof present in the worktree; they were not executed, as required.

The five scoring components are each worth two points:

1. Catalog and rules fidelity.
2. IR trace.
3. Observable behavior-driving proof.
4. Peer and evolution-stack proof.
5. Executed delivery gates.

Component 5 is fixed at `0/2` for every card because this pass explicitly prohibits tests, typecheck, lint, formatters, browser checks, collection/mechanism gates, and `git diff --check`. Thus the maximum is provisional `8/10`; structural assertions and direct/manual timing or event injection were not counted as full behavioral proof where a natural public origin was available. BT14-064 is additionally `7/10 provisional` because the natural fixture proves its On Play and inherited watcher paths but does not naturally exercise the separate When Digivolving path.

The shared seams inspected were:

- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/registration/module.ts` and the card registry for compiled module normalization, trigger routing, inherited-effect collection, and exclusive registration.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/runAction.ts` for optional activation, mandatory-after-activation costs, trigger dispatch, and frequency handling.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/costs.ts` and `apps/api/src/engine/effects/primitives.ts` for return/trash costs, deck movement, deletion, De-Digivolve, memory, and battle leave processing.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/reveal.ts` for reveal ownership, RevealAdd disposition, free play, play-cost budgets, revealed-card scaling, and top/bottom restoration.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/removal.ts` for DeleteBudget and deletion target/cost accumulation.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/digivolution.ts` for repeated De-Digivolve dispatch.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/continuous.ts`, `actions/subTrigger.ts`, and `effects/subtriggers.ts` for turn-scoped grants, inherited watchers, `OncePerTurn`, opponent-effect restrictions, and deletion subjects.
- `apps/api/src/engine/combat/controller.ts`, `actions/attack.ts`, and the testkit `harness.ts`/`advance.ts`/`observe.ts` for natural play, evolution, battle deletion, block windows, and real turn-end windows.

All ten direct modules have `coverage: "full"`, `residual: []`, and exactly one executable registration of the form `registerIrCard("BT14-NNN", compiled)`. No audited module introduces or preserves a duplicate `registerCard` registration.

#### internal-docs/audits/BT14/BT14-101-102.md

##### Immutable generated-effects note

The immutable `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` snapshot agrees with BT14-101 at lines
28079–28172. Its BT14-102 record at lines 28174–28257 is stale in three behaviorally material ways:

1. The modal has no `deleteOwn` cost, `optional`, or `abortOnDecline` fields.
2. The direct On Deletion hatch is represented as `PlaceInBattleAreaSelf` in the breeding area rather
   than the executable `Hatch` action.
3. The inherited hand placement lacks the direct module's `optional: true`.

The direct card modules are the executable IR authority: `registerIrCard` writes the normalized direct
record into `compiledEffects` and `registeredCompiledCards` before creating the runtime module
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/registration/module.ts:323-340`). The stale shared snapshot
was therefore documented, not edited, per task scope.

- Contradiction: the static pass of 2026-09-05 scored every card at most 8/10 provisional, with executed gates at 0/2, and scored these lower still:

  The 2026-09-10 ledger supersedes it because the gates it withheld were then executed and the flagged proofs were strengthened. The static rows are kept per card so the earlier reservation stays visible.

- Contradiction: `docs/audits/BT14-AUDIT.md` (2026-09-02) declared the set complete at 10/10 before the 2026-09-10 independent re-audit; its own text names the files it superseded. Its counts differ from the re-audit's because the collections were rerun (see both gate blocks above).
- Deferred commands: the static pass deliberately ran no tests, typecheck, lint, format, browser check, or `git diff --check`. Nothing from it remains deferred; the 2026-09-10 run executed those gates.
- Raw evidence (logs and command output) was not retained in the tree. The reported commands and counts above are the record; the bytes stay in git history at commit `eabe99351` and earlier.
